ROUTE-ORDER ANALYSIS

- Lines 15-35 install an in-memory auth rate limiter before request handling.
- The HTTP router is a single ordered chain inside/near the main server handler; earlier exact handlers for auth/admin/API routes run before final static serving and SPA fallback.
- Static serving and SPA fallback occur near lines 8158-8353, after most special routes. This means route order matters heavily: a new special route added below the static block may be unreachable or treated as static/SPA.
- Admin routes are guarded by admin mode readiness and `isAdminAuthed` logic; `/__admin/login` and disabled-owner-tools responses are expected exceptions.
- `/__auth/login` appears very late at lines 8042-8104, but still before final static fallback, so it is reachable.

Static-serving conclusions:
- URL path is normalized with `path.normalize(urlPath)` and joined to `PUBLIC_DIR`, but direct evidence of a robust `startsWith(PUBLIC_DIR)` symlink/escape check should be reviewed before trusting traversal protection.
- Runtime glue/cache-sensitive files use `cacheHeaderForRequest()`: root/index/runtime JS/sync/service worker receive no-store, while hashed assets may receive immutable.
- `sw.js` sets `Service-Worker-Allowed: /` during static send.
- Gzip caching is limited to hashed assets through `_gzipCache`; runtime-patched assets avoid immutable caching.
- Range request/media streaming support was not confirmed in the static evidence; large media may be served whole-file.
- HEAD handling is not clearly separated in the extracted static path; verify with curl HEAD tests.

Auth conclusions:
- Browser auth/session compatibility is handled by injected helper code plus auth bridge ecosystem keys: `sb-projectRef-auth-token`, `isotope-auth-token`, `isotope-last-jwt`, `isotope-last-rt`, and `isotope-last-session-raw`.
- `/__auth/login` performs Supabase password login, maps username to `@isotope.local` where needed, fetches a profile bundle, and returns session/user data.
- Auth route rate limiting is IP-keyed for login attempts.
- Refresh-token handling exists in injected browser helper code; failure becomes an auth-block state instead of claiming sync success.

Admin conclusions:
- Admin mode is feature-gated by `ENABLE_ADMIN_MODE`, service-role presence, and admin cookie secret readiness.
- Admin unlock accepts either `ADMIN_SECRET` or a Supabase session verified through `/auth/v1/user` plus email/role checks.
- The `iso_admin` cookie value is deterministic HMAC over a fixed string using `ADMIN_SECRET` or service-role key; compromise of that secret grants owner-tool access.
- Owner tools expose schema patch/apply operations and must stay local/operator-only.

Supabase conclusions:
- Public anon key is used for client/bootstrap/auth-compatible flows.
- Service-role key is intended only for owner/admin/server-side maintenance, storage bucket creation, Management API SQL apply, and backfills.
- Code references many public tables/storage buckets; schema drift remains a direct risk when live Supabase lacks those objects or policies.

Backup/restore conclusions:
- Server and injected browser helper both normalize/merge backup data.
- Empty-overwrite and stale-state protections must be tested at the boundary between browser local stores, `/__auth/backup/*`, and Supabase storage.
- Backup routes are auth-sensitive and should never report `ok: true` after auth failure.

- In-memory rate limiter and gzip cache reset on process restart.
- Admin/backfill startup tasks are gated by admin mode, but idempotency depends on Supabase response handling.
- Static patched bundles are generated in memory and served on demand; stale PWA/browser caches are mitigated by no-store runtime cache policy.

DOCUMENTATION DRIFT

- README/ADMIN/CHANGELOG reference server behavior extensively; route and schema claims should be validated against this addendum and `audit/_meta/route-map.txt`.
- Prior docs mention removed service-role JWT material; current static audit still sees service-role variable usage but not hard-coded service-role values.
- The server contains many runtime patching comments; docs should warn that patched bundle logic must be tested in browser, not only by Node syntax checks.

SECURITY CONCERNS BY CATEGORY

- Authentication bypass: no direct bypass confirmed, but admin unlock combines secret, cookie, and Supabase-role paths; tests should cover all failure branches.
- Authorisation mistakes: service-role operations are admin-gated by intent; verify every `/__admin/*` mutating route calls `isAdminAuthed` or disabled response before doing work.
- Exposed credentials: public anon/default values are browser-exposed by design; service-role must remain env-only/server-only. Add regression tests around public HTML/JS responses.
- Unsafe cookies: `iso_admin` is deterministic HMAC; confirm Set-Cookie uses HttpOnly/SameSite/Secure where practical for local use.
- Path traversal: static path normalization exists, but explicit symlink/root escape hardening was not proven by this static pass.
- Injection/XSS: admin/login HTML uses `escapeHtml()` in visible dynamic fields; injected key/auth scripts intentionally emit config into HTML and need redaction boundaries.
- CSRF: mutating POST admin routes should be treated as CSRF-relevant because cookie auth exists.
- SSRF: proxy/admin SQL/API routes use configured Supabase/AI endpoints; avoid user-controlled arbitrary upstream hosts.
- RLS gaps: app correctness depends on Supabase RLS/policies matching code paths; audit did not query live Supabase.
- Backup privacy: backup payloads may include personal study/profile data; storage bucket policies and server auth checks are critical.

PERFORMANCE CONCERNS

- `server.mjs` is 8,616 lines and 440,665 bytes, creating a high parse/review burden.
- Whole-file static serving and gzip compression can be request-path expensive for non-hashed/runtime assets.
- Large media and vendor bundles are documented in performance meta reports.
- Repeated startup/backfill Supabase calls can slow boot when admin mode is enabled.
- Browser-injected sync helper is large and stringified inside server code, coupling server parse cost to frontend runtime logic.

MONOLITH DECOMPOSITION PROPOSAL

- `server/config.mjs`: dotenv loading, env validation, public config construction.
- `server/static.mjs`: MIME types, cache headers, gzip cache, SPA fallback, traversal-safe static serving.
- `server/auth.mjs`: Supabase token verification, login/signup/bootstrap/profile, session helpers.
- `server/admin.mjs`: admin unlock, cookie handling, admin route guards, owner-tool pages.
- `server/supabase-rest.mjs`: anon/service REST wrappers, timeout/error normalization.
- `server/backup-routes.mjs`: backup upload/latest/best/snapshot/restore endpoints.
- `server/proxy-routes.mjs`: AI/proxy endpoints with explicit upstream allowlists.
- `server/startup.mjs`: storage bucket checks, startup backfills, pre-gzip.

DEDICATED SERVER FINDINGS

- finding ID: ISO-AUDIT-SERVER-DEEP-01
  severity: HIGH
  category: security
  status: CONFIRMED
  confidence: HIGH
  affected lines or byte-level evidence: service-role variables and admin operations are referenced in server.mjs; see Supabase/admin maps above
  observed evidence: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, admin mode, Management API SQL apply, storage bucket setup, and backfills are all present
  inferred impact: if admin gating regresses, privileged Supabase operations could be exposed from the local server
  reproduction or reasoning: inspect `/__admin/*` route ranges and verify guard order before each mutating operation
  recommended correction: add automated route tests proving unauthenticated admin mutations fail and public HTML/JS never contains service-role values
  related files: .env.example, ADMIN.md, sql/**
  regression risk: medium; owner tooling and normal app serving share one monolithic file

- finding ID: ISO-AUDIT-SERVER-DEEP-02
  severity: MEDIUM
  category: correctness/data-loss
  status: CONFIRMED
  confidence: HIGH
  affected lines or byte-level evidence: backup/restore/sync maps above show many backup and cloud snapshot flows
  observed evidence: server and injected browser helper both manipulate backup snapshots, hashes, metadata, and auth-block state
  inferred impact: an auth failure, empty backup, or schema drift can leave sync pending or risk wrong restore behavior
  reproduction or reasoning: exercise `/__auth/backup/*`, `/__auth/bootstrap`, and browser sync with expired JWT, empty local data, and missing table fixtures
  recommended correction: keep auth failure as a hard stop and add fixture tests for empty-overwrite and IDB/localStorage divergence
  related files: public/sync/local-data-adapter.js, public/sync/backup-normalizer.js, public/assets/App-pJGjDiPw.js
  regression risk: high because sync spans browser storage, server routes, and Supabase storage

- finding ID: ISO-AUDIT-SERVER-DEEP-03
  severity: MEDIUM
  category: maintainability
  status: CONFIRMED
  confidence: HIGH
  affected lines or byte-level evidence: server.mjs has 8616 lines and embeds server routing, static serving, admin tools, browser helper JS, sync, and startup backfills
  observed evidence: complete section/function maps above
  inferred impact: small runtime-glue changes can accidentally affect unrelated admin/static/sync behavior
  reproduction or reasoning: review route order and injected script sections; unrelated concerns are co-located
  recommended correction: decompose into focused modules only after route/cache/auth regression tests are locked
  related files: server/backup-manager.mjs, public/sync/backup-normalizer.js
  regression risk: medium during decomposition

- finding ID: ISO-AUDIT-SERVER-DEEP-04
  severity: LOW
  category: static-serving
  status: INFERRED
  confidence: MEDIUM
  affected lines or byte-level evidence: static serving map around lines 8158-8353
  observed evidence: path normalization and public-dir join are present; explicit symlink/root escape proof was not found in extracted evidence
  inferred impact: traversal/symlink handling should be proven by tests even if current path normalization blocks common `..` inputs
  reproduction or reasoning: run static tests for encoded traversal, null bytes, symlink escape, HEAD, missing files, and range requests
  recommended correction: add explicit `path.resolve(filePath).startsWith(PUBLIC_DIR + path.sep)` style guard and tests if not already equivalent
  related files: public/**
  regression risk: low if tests are added first
